                [32m"* Convert every `*.html` fixture in a given directory with Docling's HTML backend, using the backend's **default** HTML backend options, and record structural facts about each resulting document: two markdown views (BODY content layer only, and BODY plus FURNITURE), table count, table shapes, table cell texts, and picture count.\n"[39m +
                [32m'* Run a fixed matrix of **resource-location resolutions** through the same resolution logic the HTML backend uses for locations found in a document, and record for each probe whether the location was resolved (and to what) or rejected (and with which error).\n'[39m +
                [32m"* Run a fixed matrix of **image-data loads** through the backend's image-loading logic under specific fetch switches, and record whether the data was loaded (and its size), skipped without data, or blocked (and with which error).\n"[39m +
                [32m"* Run a fixed set of URLs through the backend's remote-URL safety validation and record which are accepted and which are refused.\n"[39m +
                [32m'* Aggregate everything into a single JSON report.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* All facts in the report must be produced by actually exercising Docling in the current environment. The tool is a general audit tool: it will be pointed at different fixture directories and different local resource trees, and it must produce the correct report for whatever it is given.\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'## Implementation Hints\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Project path: `/home/user/project`\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Everything needed is already installed; do not install packages. The audit tool itself must not perform any network I/O.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Fixture corpus (already present): `/home/user/project/assets/html/` contains the `*.html` files to audit.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Local resource tree (already present): `/home/user/project/assets/local/` contains `page.html`, `images/tiny.png` and `images/diagram.svg`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Command (rerunnable): `python main.py --html-dir <html_dir> --local-root <local_root> --report <report_path>`\n'[39m +
                [32m'  * It will be invoked from the project directory as:\n'[39m +
                [32m'    `python main.py --html-dir assets/html --local-root assets/local --report output/report.json`\n'[39m +
                [32m'  * All three flags are required. Paths may be relative to the working directory or absolute. Create parent directories of the report if they do not exist. On success, exit with code `0`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'  * If `--html-dir` or `--local-root` does not exist as a directory, write no report, print a diagnostic to stderr, and exit with code `2`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* Two named base locations are used by the probe matrices below:\n'[39m +
                [32m'  * `LOCAL_BASE` — the **absolute** path of `<local_root>/page.html`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'  * `REMOTE_BASE` — the literal string `https://cdn.example.com/docs/page.html`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'### Report format\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'The report is a single UTF-8 JSON object with exactly these top-level keys, in this order: `documents`, `resolution_probes`, `fetch_probes`, `url_safety_probes`, `summary`.\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'**`documents`** — one object per `*.html` file located directly in `<html_dir>` (non-recursive), ordered ascending by file name (Unicode code-point order). Each object has exactly these keys, in this order:\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `name` (string): the file name including its extension.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `body_lines` (array of strings): take the document\'s markdown export restricted to the BODY content layer; split it on `"\\n"`; right-strip every line; drop every line that is empty after stripping.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `body_blocks` (array of strings): take the same BODY markdown; split it on `"\\n\\n"`; strip every element; drop every element that is empty after stripping.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `full_lines` (array of strings): identical derivation to `body_lines`, but computed from the markdown export that includes **both** the BODY and the FURNITURE content layers.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `tables` (integer): the number of tables in the converted document.\n'[39m +
                [32m'* `table_shapes` (array): one `[num_rows, num_cols]` integer pair per table, in document order.\n'[39m +
                [32m"* `table_cell_texts` (array): one array per table, in document order, containing the text of each of that table's cells verbatim (in the table's own cell order, no trimming, no substitutions).\n"[39m +
                [32m'* `pictures` (integer): the number of picture items in the converted document.\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'**`resolution_probes`** — one object per probe below, ordered ascending by `id`. Each probe resolves the given location against the given base location exactly as the HTML backend resolves locations it encounters in a document. Keys, in this order: `id` (string), `outcome` (string, `"resolved"` or `"rejected"`), `resolved` (the returned location string when resolved, otherwise `null`), `error_type` (the raised exception\'s class name when rejected, otherwise `null`), `error_message` (the raised exception rendered as a string when rejected, otherwise `null`).\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `id`                   | location                         | base location |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| ---------------------- | -------------------------------- | ------------- |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `abs_posix`            | `/etc/passwd`                    | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `file_uri`             | `file:///etc/passwd`             | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `fragment`             | `#section-2`                     | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `local_relative`       | `images/tiny.png`                | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `protocol_relative`    | `//cdn.example.com/img/a.png`    | `REMOTE_BASE` |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `remote_relative`      | `img/a.png`                      | `REMOTE_BASE` |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `remote_root_relative` | `/img/a.png`                     | `REMOTE_BASE` |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `traversal`            | `../../../../etc/passwd`         | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `traversal_sneaky`     | `images/../../../../etc/passwd`  | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `windows_drive`        | `C:\\Windows\\System32\\config\\sam` | `LOCAL_BASE`  |\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'**`fetch_probes`** — one object per probe below, ordered ascending by `id`. Each probe hands the given source string, **as-is** (it is not resolved again), to the backend\'s image-data loading step, with the given base location and the given switches in effect. Any switch not mentioned keeps its default value. Keys, in this order: `id` (string), `outcome` (string: `"loaded"` if image bytes were returned, `"skipped"` if the load completed without returning data and without raising, `"blocked"` if it raised), `num_bytes` (integer length of the returned bytes when loaded, otherwise `null`), `error_type` (the raised exception\'s class name when blocked, otherwise `null`), `error_message` (the raised exception rendered as a string when blocked, otherwise `null`).\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `id`                 | source                                                                                                        | base location | switches                                            |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `data_uri_ok`        | `data:image/png;base64,` followed by the base64 encoding of the exact bytes of `<local_root>/images/tiny.png` | none          | maximum accepted base64 image payload: `1024` bytes |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `data_uri_too_large` | `data:image/png;base64,` followed by the base64 encoding of 2048 NUL bytes (`b"\\x00" * 2048`)                 | none          | maximum accepted base64 image payload: `1024` bytes |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `local_disabled`     | the absolute path of `<local_root>/images/tiny.png`                                                           | `LOCAL_BASE`  | defaults only                                       |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `local_enabled`      | the absolute path of `<local_root>/images/tiny.png`                                                           | `LOCAL_BASE`  | local resource fetching enabled                     |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `local_no_base_path` | `images/tiny.png`                                                                                             | none          | local resource fetching enabled                     |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `remote_disabled`    | `https://cdn.example.com/img/a.png`                                                                           | none          | defaults only                                       |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `svg_skipped`        | the absolute path of `<local_root>/images/diagram.svg`                                                        | `LOCAL_BASE`  | local resource fetching enabled                     |\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'**`url_safety_probes`** — one object per probe below, ordered ascending by `id`. Each probe submits the URL to the backend\'s remote-URL safety validation. Keys, in this order: `id` (string), `url` (string, exactly as listed), `outcome` (string, `"allowed"` if validation passed, `"rejected"` if it raised), `error_type` (the raised exception\'s class name when rejected, otherwise `null`), `error_message` (the raised exception rendered as a string when rejected, otherwise `null`).\n'[39m +
                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'| `id`                  | url                                       |\n'[39m +
                [32m'| -----'[39m... 1451 more characters
            }

                    [32m'                    colalign.append("right" if col_type in (int, float) else "left")\r\n'[39m +
